<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every elliptic curve over ℚ, we prove that the average analytic rank of its quadratic twists tends to 1/2 when signed squarefree twist parameters are ordered by absolute value. This resolves Goldfeld's mean analytic-rank conjecture in this counting convention.

- **claim-mean-analytic-rank** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-mean)): For every elliptic curve $E/\mathbb Q$, $$\lim_{Y\to\infty}\frac{1}{\#\mathcal D(Y)} \sum_{d\in\mathcal D(Y)}a(E^{(d)})=\frac12.$$
  - Explanation: For every elliptic curve over the rationals, the average analytic rank over signed squarefree quadratic twists ordered by absolute value converges to one half.
- **claim-rank-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-tail)): For every elliptic curve $E/\mathbb Q$, there are constants $C_E>0$ and an integer $R_E\ge1$ such that, for every integer $R\ge R_E$, $$\limsup_{Y\to\infty}\frac1Y \sum_{\substack{d\in\mathcal D(Y)\\a(E^{(d)})>R}}a(E^{(d)}) \le \frac{C_E}{R}.$$
  - Explanation: The rank mass carried by twists of analytic rank above a threshold R has limsup density bounded by a constant over R, so rare large-rank twists do not affect the mean.
- **claim-algebraic-moments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-algebraic-moments)): Fix an elliptic curve $E/\mathbb Q$. For every fixed real $t$, $$\lim_{Y\to\infty}\frac1{\#\mathcal D(Y)} \sum_{d\in\mathcal D(Y)}\exp\!\bigl(t\,r(E^{(d)})\bigr) =\frac{1+e^t}{2}.$$ For every fixed positive integer $m$, $$\lim_{Y\to\infty}\frac1{\#\mathcal D(Y)} \sum_{d\in\mathcal D(Y)}r(E^{(d)})^m=\frac12.$$
  - Explanation: Fixed exponential and power moments of the algebraic (Mordell–Weil) rank over the same twist family have explicit limits determined by ranks zero and one.
- **lemma-reduction-admissible** (theorem, [source](#stmt-setup-reduction)): For every $F\in\mathcal F$ and signed squarefree $b$, there are $F'\in\mathcal F$ and an admissible $d$ such that $$F^{(b)}\simeq (F')^{(d)},\qquad \|d\|\le \|b\|.$$ The correspondence can be chosen to have bounded multiplicity, uniformly in $b$. Every sum of nonnegative functions of these twists can therefore be bounded by a fixed multiple of the corresponding sums over $\mathcal F$ and admissible parameters of no greater height.
  - Explanation: Every twist of a curve in the finite family is isomorphic to a twist of a curve in the family by an admissible parameter of no larger height, with bounded multiplicity.
- **lemma-twist-local-factors** (theorem, [source](#stmt-setup-local-factors)): For $F\in\mathcal F$ and admissible $d$, $$\label{eq:setup-twist} L_{F^{(d)}}(s)=\sum_{n\ge1}\lambda_F(n)\chi_d(n)n^{-s}, \qquad q_{F^{(d)}}=q_F\|d\|^2, \qquad \chi_d(n)=\left(\frac dn\right).$$ Here the symbol is the Kronecker symbol and $d=1$ has the untwisted convention. For an arbitrary signed squarefree $b$, the same good-prime twisting rule holds at primes outside $S$: the local trace is multiplied by $(b/p)$ when $p\nmid b$, and the local factor is one when $p\mid b$.
  - Explanation: For admissible twists the Dirichlet series is the character-twisted series and the conductor is multiplied by the square of the twist parameter.
- **prop-log-second-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-analytic-moment)): There are fixed positive integers $A,B$ such that, for every dyadic $M\geq1$, every $F\in\mathcal F$, every $t\in\mathbb R$, and $1/2\leq\sigma\leq2$, $$\label{analytic:L-moment} \frac1M\sum_{\substack{d\ \mathrm{admissible}\\M\leq\|d\|<2M}} \|L_{F^{(d)}}(\sigma+it)\|^2 \ll_{\mathcal F}\log^B(3M)(1+\|t\|)^A.$$ For arbitrary signed squarefree twisting parameters and $H\geq1$, $$\label{analytic:prefix-moment} \sum_{\substack{0<\|h\|\leq H\\h\ \mathrm{squarefree}}} \|L_{F^{(h)}}(\sigma+it)\|^2 \ll_{\mathcal F}H\log^B(3H)(1+\|t\|)^A.$$
  - Explanation: Second moments of the twisted L-functions over admissible and over arbitrary squarefree parameters lose only powers of a logarithm and polynomial growth in the vertical parameter.
- **prop-polynomial-second-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-analytic-polynomial-moment)): With the same fixed integers $A,B$, every fixed smooth $G$ of compact support in $(0,\infty)$ satisfies $$\label{analytic:polynomial-bound} \frac1M\sum_{\substack{d\ \mathrm{admissible}\\M\leq\|d\|<2M}} \|P_d(N,t;G)\|^2 \ll_{\mathcal F,G} \log^B(3M)(1+\|t\|)^A(1+N/M)^A \qquad(N>0).$$ The bound is uniform for fixed uniformly smooth families of weights with common compact support.
  - Explanation: Smoothly localized Dirichlet polynomials satisfy the same logarithmic second-moment bounds, with a polynomial dependence on the ratio of the length to the height.
- **lemma-gauss-sums** (theorem, [source](#stmt-analytic-gauss)): For fixed $h$, the function $l\mapsto B_h(l)$ is multiplicative on odd positive integers, and $$\label{analytic:phase} B_{-h}(l)=\left(\frac{-1}{l}\right)B_h(l),\qquad \epsilon_l B_h(l)=\frac{1+i}{2}B_h(l)+\frac{1-i}{2}B_{-h}(l).$$ If $h\ne0$, $p$ is odd, $t=v_p(h)$, and $v\geq1$, then $$\label{analytic:prime-powers} B_h(p^v)= \begin{cases} p^{v/2}(1-p^{-1}),&v\text{ even},\ v\leq t,\\ -p^{v/2-1},&v\text{ even},\ v=t+1,\\ p^{(v-1)/2}\left(\dfrac{h/p^{v-1}}p\right), &v\text{ odd},\ v=t+1,\\ 0,&\text{otherwise}. \end{cases}$$ Consequently $B_h(p^v)=0$ for $v>t+1$ and $\|B_h(p^v)\|\leq p^{v/2}$. If $t=0$, only $v=1$ can survive and $B_h(p)=(h/p)$. If $t=1$, only $v=2$ can survive and $B_h(p^2)=-1$.
  - Explanation: The normalized quadratic Gauss sums are multiplicative, satisfy a sign-flip identity, and have explicit prime-power values with a support condition.
- **lemma-euler-factorization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-analytic-euler)): Let $r$ be positive and odd, and write $h=h_1h_2^2\ne0$, where $h_1$ is signed squarefree and $h_2\geq1$. For $\nu\in\{1,-1\}$, the series $$\mathcal G_{h,r}^{\nu}(s_1,s_2)= \sum_{\substack{n_1,n_2\geq1\\n_1,n_2\ \mathrm{odd}}} \frac{\lambda_F(n_1)\lambda_F(n_2)B_{\nu h}(rn_1n_2)} {n_1^{s_1}n_2^{s_2}}$$ initially converges in a right half-plane and continues to $\Re s_1,\Re s_2>1/2$ as $$\label{analytic:euler-factorization} \mathcal G_{h,r}^{\nu}(s_1,s_2)= L_{F^{(\nu h_1)}}(s_1)L_{F^{(\nu h_1)}}(s_2) \mathcal Q_{h,r}^{\nu}(s_1,s_2).$$ For $0<b\leq1/4$ and $\Re s_i\geq1/2+b$, $$\label{analytic:euler-bound} \|\mathcal Q_{h,r}^{\nu}(s_1,s_2)\| \ll_{\mathcal F} b^{-C_{\rm ar}}r^{C_{\rm ar}} \tau(h_2)^{C_{\rm ar}}.$$ The function $\mathcal Q$ is holomorphic in these open half-planes.
  - Explanation: The two-variable series built from Gauss sums factors as a product of two twist L-functions and an explicitly bounded holomorphic Euler product.
- **lemma-poisson-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-analytic-poisson)): Let $U>0$, let $r$ be odd and positive, and let $N_i$ be bounded below by a fixed positive constant. Fix $0<b\leq1/4$. In the expression $$\label{analytic:poisson-average} \frac1U\sum_{m\in\mathbb Z} \sum_{\substack{n_1,n_2\geq1\\n_1,n_2\ \mathrm{odd}}} \frac{\lambda_F(n_1)\lambda_F(n_2)} {(n_1n_2)^{1/2}n_1^{it_1}n_2^{it_2}} \left(\frac m{rn_1n_2}\right) W(n_1/N_1,n_2/N_2,m/U),$$ Poisson summation gives the zero frequency, namely the complete-residue average integrated in $x$, together with the nonzero frequencies. For every fixed $J_1>0$, a sufficiently large fixed seminorm order $D$ bounds the absolute contribution of frequency $h=h_1h_2^2\ne0$ by $$\begin{aligned} &C_{J_1,\mathcal F}\\|W\\|_{D,*} (N_1N_2)^{-1/2+b}b^{-C_{\rm ar}}r^{C_{\rm ar}} \tau(h_2)^{C_{\rm ar}} \left(1+\frac{\|h\|U}{rN_1N_2}\right)^{-J_1} \nonumber\\[-2pt] &\hspace{8mm}\cdot \sum_{\nu=\pm1}\int_{\mathbb R^2} \frac{\prod_{i=1}^2 \|L_{F^{(\nu h_1)}}(1/2+b+i(t_i+v_i))\|} {(1+\|v_1\|+\|v_2\|)^{J_1}}\,dv_1\,dv_2. \label{analytic:nonzero-mode} \end{aligned}$$ If the Fourier transform of $W$ in $x$ is supported in a fixed interval $[-C_W,C_W]$, all modes with $\|h\|>C_Wa_1^2rN_1N_2/U$ vanish exactly.
  - Explanation: Poisson summation splits the weighted average into a complete-residue zero-frequency term and nonzero frequencies bounded by twist L-functions, vanishing beyond a Fourier-support threshold.
- **lemma-dual-frequency-sum** (bound, [source](#stmt-analytic-frequency-sum)): For fixed $B\geq0$, $J_1>1$, and $Q>0$, $$\label{analytic:dyadic-frequency-bound} \sum_{T=1,2,4,\ldots}T\log^B(3T)(1+T/Q)^{-J_1} \ll_{B,J_1} \begin{cases} Q^{J_1},&0<Q<1,\\ Q\log^B(3Q),&Q\geq1. \end{cases}$$ In particular the first bound is at most a constant times $Q$.
  - Explanation: Summing over all dyadic dual frequencies gives two explicit regimes depending on whether the scale parameter is below or above one.
- **prop-model-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-analytic-comparison)): Let $\ell=\log X$, where $X$ is sufficiently large, and let $$c_1\ell^{-1/10}\leq\Delta\leq\frac12$$ for a fixed $c_1>0$. There are fixed constants $\eta,c_2>0$ for which the following holds. Let $$S_V(x)=\sum_{n\geq1}\frac{\lambda_F(n)V(n)}{\sqrt n}\, w_x(\ell^{-1}\log n),\qquad H_V=\sum_{r'\leq R_0}h(r')V(r'),$$ where $V=z_m$ or $Z$. Suppose that $S_V$ has length at most $X^{1-\Delta}$, that $$\label{analytic:multiplier-conditions} 1\leq R_0\leq X^{\eta\Delta},\qquad \sum_{r'\leq R_0}\|h(r')\|\leq C_H R_0^2,$$ and that $w_x$ and a function $A(x)$ have bounds by a fixed power of $\ell$ for all derivatives up to a sufficiently large fixed order. For $w_x$ these bounds are required after smooth dyadic localization in $n$, on the scaled variables $n/N$ and $x$ in a fixed neighborhood of $\operatorname{supp}\Psi$. They may in particular follow from bounds in $u=\ell^{-1}\log n\geq0$ and $x$, with a smooth extension at $n=1$. Then $$\begin{aligned} &\frac1X\sum_{m\in\mathbb Z}\Psi(m/X) \|H_{z_m}S_{z_m}(m/X)-A(m/X)\|^2 \nonumber\\ &\hspace{8mm}= \int_{\mathbb R}\Psi(x)\, \mathbb E\|H_ZS_Z(x)-A(x)\|^2\,dx +O\big(\exp(-c_2\Delta\ell)\big). \label{analytic:model-comparison} \end{aligned}$$ The constants and the sufficiently large $X$ threshold are uniform when the stated support, coefficient, and derivative bounds are uniform. They do not depend on $\Delta$ or on an index parametrizing such weights. One may take $$\label{analytic:comparison-constants} C'=C_{\rm ar}+1=257,\qquad \eta=\frac1{4(4+2C')},\qquad c_2=\frac12.$$
  - Explanation: Mean squares of short mollified Dirichlet polynomials over integers agree with the independent random model up to an exponentially small error in the length-saving parameter.
- **lemma-completed-derivative-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-weights-vanishing)): Let $d$ be admissible, $X\le \|d\|<2X$, and $x=d/X$. Set $$g_d(z)=L_{F^{(d)}}(1/2+z)(C_F\|d\|)^z\Gamma(1+z), \qquad \epsilon_d=\operatorname{sign}(F^{(d)}).$$ Then $g_d$ is entire, $g_d(-z)=\epsilon_d g_d(z)$, and $$\begin{split} \mathcal S_d &:= \sum_{n\ge1}\frac{\lambda(n)\chi_d(n)}{\sqrt n} W_{k,x}(\ell^{-1}\log n)\\ &=\frac{k!}{\ell^k}\frac{1}{2\pi i} \int_{(2)}g_d(z)\frac{dz}{z^{k+1}}. \end{split} \label{weights:completed-integral}$$ More precisely, $$\bigl(1+\epsilon_d(-1)^k\bigr)\mathcal S_d =\ell^{-k}g_d^{(k)}(0). \label{weights:exact-derivative}$$ Consequently, if $a(F^{(d)})>k$ and $\epsilon_d=(-1)^k$, then $$\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{\lambda(n)\chi_d(n)}{\sqrt n} W_{k,d/X}(\ell^{-1}\log n)=0. \label{weights:zero}$$
  - Explanation: An exact contour-integral identity expresses the smoothed Dirichlet sum through a derivative of the completed L-function, so it vanishes when the rank exceeds the derivative order with matching sign.
- **lemma-weight-derivative-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-weights-derivatives)): Fix an integer $r\ge0$, and then take $k_0\ge r+2$. For $a,b\ge0$, $a+b\le r$, uniformly in <a href="#weights:range" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="weights:range">[weights:range]</a>, $x\in\mathcal K$, and the indicated arguments, one has $$\begin{aligned} \|\partial_u^a\partial_x^b w_{j,x}(u)\| &\le C_r(k/\delta_j)^r\delta_j^k &&(1\le j\le J,\ u\in\mathbb R), \label{weights:short-derivatives}\\ \|\partial_u^a\partial_x^b w_{0,x}(u)\| &\le C_r k^r &&(u\ge-\log2/\ell). \label{weights:main-derivatives} \end{aligned}$$ There is also $c>0$, independent of $r,k,X$, such that, with $\delta=\delta_J$ and $n=e^{\ell u}>0$, $$\|\partial_u^a\partial_x^b w_{*,x}(u)\| \le C_r(k/\delta)^r\delta^k e^{-cn/X}. \label{weights:terminal-derivatives}$$ Changing the fixed compact neighborhood of $\mathcal K$ changes only the constants.
  - Explanation: Fixed-order derivative bounds hold for the short pieces, the main piece and the terminal piece of the partitioned weight, uniformly in the allowed range.
- **cor-comparison-interface** (theorem, [source](#stmt-weights-comparison-interface)): For every fixed derivative order $r$, the short weights satisfy the smoothness assumptions of Proposition <a href="#analytic:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="analytic:comparison">12</a> with derivative bounds $\ell^{C_r}$, uniformly in $j,k,X$. They have length $X^{1-\Delta_j}$, with $$\Delta_j=\delta_j/4,\qquad \tfrac14\ell^{-1/10}\le\Delta_j\le\tfrac14 .$$ The deterministic term $$s=k/\ell,\qquad A_x=(C_F\|x\|)^s\Gamma(1+s) \label{weights:Ax}$$ has uniformly bounded derivatives of each fixed order on $\mathcal K$, and is bounded above and below by positive constants. The endpoint enlargement at $n=1$ can be made inside the fixed range $n\ge1/2$.
  - Explanation: The short weights meet the smoothness and length hypotheses of the model comparison, and the deterministic main term is bounded above and below.
- **prop-terminal-mean-square** (bound, [source](#stmt-weights-terminal)): There is a fixed exponent $C_T$, chosen before $k_0$, such that uniformly in <a href="#weights:range" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="weights:range">[weights:range]</a>, $$\frac1X\sum_{\substack{d\ {\rm admissible}\\X\le\|d\|<2X}} \|S_{*,\chi_d}(d/X)\|^2 \ll \ell^{C_T}\delta_J^{2k}. \label{weights:terminal-square}$$ The same estimate holds after restricting the summation to either value of $\chi_d(2)$.
  - Explanation: The unmollified terminal series has mean square bounded by a power of the logarithm times a high power of the last scale width.
- **lemma-terminal-absorption** (bound, [source](#stmt-weights-terminal-small)): After increasing the fixed $k_0$, one sufficiently large lower bound on $X$ makes $$\frac1X\sum_{\substack{d\ {\rm admissible}\\X\le\|d\|<2X}} \|S_{*,\chi_d}(d/X)\|^2 \ll 2^{-3Jk/2} \label{weights:terminal-small-square}$$ simultaneously for all $k$ in <a href="#weights:range" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="weights:range">[weights:range]</a>. Moreover, for any fixed $c,L>0$, the comparison errors obey $$e^{-c\Delta_j\ell} \le k^{-L}2^{-Ljk}\qquad(0\le j\le J) \label{weights:comparison-absorption}$$ for one sufficiently large lower bound on $X$.
  - Explanation: After enlarging the base derivative order, the terminal mean square is geometrically small and the comparison errors are absorbed uniformly over all pieces.
- **lemma-mollifier-length** (bound, [source](#stmt-model-mollifier-length)): The polynomial $H_j$ has an expansion $$H_j(V)=\sum_{r\le R_j}h_j(r)V(r),\qquad \|h_j(r)\|\le\tau(r)r^{-1/2},\qquad \sum_r\|h_j(r)\|\ll R_j^2,$$ where, for a fixed constant $C_{\rm len}>0$, one may take $$R_j=\exp\left(C_{\rm len}(k^{1/4}+j+1) \ell\delta_j^2/\sqrt k\right). \label{model:length-bound}$$ For every fixed $\eta>0$, a sufficiently large fixed $k_0$ ensures $R_j\le X^{\eta\delta_j/4}$ for all $0\le j\le J$ and all $k$ in the stated range. Thus $H_j$ satisfies the multiplier hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#analytic:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="analytic:comparison">12</a>, with $\Delta=\delta_j/4$.
  - Explanation: The block mollifiers are short Dirichlet polynomials with controlled coefficients and length, satisfying the multiplier hypotheses of the model comparison.
- **lemma-prime-sum-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-model-integer-moments)): Let $\mathcal I$ be any set of primes at most $Y_0$, and put $$B_{\mathcal I}(V)=\sum_{p\in\mathcal I} \lambda(p)V(p)p^{-1/2-s}.$$ If $k_0$ is a sufficiently large absolute constant, then $$\frac1X\sum_{X\le\|m\|<2X}\|B_{\mathcal I}(z_m)\|^{64} \ll\left(1+\sum_{p\in\mathcal I}\frac1p\right)^{32} \label{model:64th-moment}$$ uniformly in $\mathcal I,k,X$.
  - Explanation: A high even moment of prime sums over integers is bounded by a power of the reciprocal prime mass, uniformly in the prime set.
- **prop-simultaneous-product-event** (bound, [source](#stmt-model-good-event)): Except for $O(Xk^{-2})$ integers in $X\le\|m\|<2X$, the following inequalities hold simultaneously: $$\begin{aligned} \frac12\le H_j(z_m)P_j^{\mathrm{eu}}(z_m)&\le2 &&(0\le j\le J), \label{model:good-mollifier}\\ \frac{P_0^{\mathrm{eu}}(z_m)}{P_j^{\mathrm{eu}}(z_m)} &\ge2^{-jk/8} &&(1\le j\le J), \label{model:good-ratio}\\ P_0^{\mathrm{eu}}(z_m)&\ge2^{-Jk/8}. \label{model:good-full-product} \end{aligned}$$ The implied constant and the lower threshold $k_0$ do not depend on $k,j,X$.
  - Explanation: Outside a small exceptional set of integers, the mollifiers approximate the positive reciprocal Euler products and the products are comparable across scales.
- **lemma-exact-shifted-products** (bound, [source](#stmt-model-exact-shifts)): Let $0<s,\alpha\le1$, $b=\alpha+it$, and $Y_j\ge2$. If $h=\alpha+s+\|t\|$ and $a=\min(\alpha,s)$, then $$\\|(P_j^{\mathrm{eu}})^{-1}\mathcal L_Q(b)\\|_2 \ll (1+h/a)^C \left(1+\frac1{\alpha\log Y_j}\right)^C. \label{model:general-exact-norm}$$ At the real point $b=s$, with $j=0$ and the parameters of this section, $$\\|(P_0^{\mathrm{eu}})^{-1}\mathcal L_Q(s)-1\\|_2^2 \ll \frac{e^{-2\sqrt k}}{\sqrt k}. \label{model:exact-distance}$$
  - Explanation: Shifted exact Euler products have controlled norms over the full model, and at the real shift the first product is exponentially close to one in mean square.
- **lemma-relative-truncation** (bound, [source](#stmt-model-relative-truncation)): For every $b$ with $\Re b>0$, uniformly in $Q\ge Y_0$, $$\\|(H_j-(P_j^{\mathrm{eu}})^{-1})\mathcal L_Q(b)\\|_2 \le \left(\exp\left(C\sum_i2^{-m_{ji}}\right)-1\right) \\|(P_j^{\mathrm{eu}})^{-1}\mathcal L_Q(b)\\|_2. \label{model:relative-error}$$ In particular the relative factor is $O(2^{-k^{1/4}-j})$.
  - Explanation: Replacing exact block products by their degree truncations costs a relative mean-square error that is exponentially small in the block degree.
- **prop-shifted-norms** (bound, [source](#stmt-model-shifted-norms)): There are fixed $C,c_0>0$ such that, uniformly for every real $\theta$ and every cutoff $Q\ge Y_0$, $$\begin{aligned} \\|H_0(Z)\mathcal L_Q(s/2+i\theta s)\\|_2 &\ll(1+\|\theta\|)^C, \label{model:half-shift}\\ \\|H_0(Z)\mathcal L_Q(s)-1\\|_2 &\ll e^{-c_0 k^{1/4}}, \label{model:mollified-distance}\\ \\|H_j(Z)\mathcal L_Q((1+i\theta)/\ell)\\|_2 &\ll (1+\|\theta\|)^C(k2^j)^C \qquad(0\le j\le J). \label{model:unit-shift} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: The mollified Euler products satisfy polynomial norm bounds at three shifts needed later, and the first is exponentially close to one.
- **lemma-global-power-approximation** (bound, [source](#stmt-model-global-power-approximation)): For each fixed nonnegative integer $r$, if $k\ge2r+4$, then for every $z\in\mathbb R$, $$\left\|\frac{d^r}{dz^r} \left((1-z/k)_+^k-e^{-z}\right)\right\| \le\frac{C_r}{k}(1+\|z\|)^2e^{-z}. \label{model:global-power-bound}$$
  - Explanation: The truncated power approximates the exponential with derivative errors of relative size one over the power, uniformly on the whole real line.
- **lemma-first-fourier-error** (bound, [source](#stmt-model-first-fourier-error)): Define $$A_x=(C_F\|x\|)^s\Gamma(1+s),\qquad E_x(y)=w_{0,x}(y/k)-A_xe^{-y}\quad(y\ge0).$$ For every required fixed integer $r$, there is an extension $g_x$ of $e^{y/2}E_x(y)$ from $y\ge0$ to $\mathbb R$ such that $$\|\widehat g_x(\theta)\|\ll_r k^{-1}(1+\|\theta\|)^{-r}. \label{model:first-Fourier-decay}$$ The constants are uniform in $x,k,X$ after choosing $k_0$ depending on $r$. The quantities $A_x$ are bounded above and below by positive fixed constants.
  - Explanation: The error between the first weight piece and its exponential profile has an extension whose Fourier transform decays rapidly with a one-over-k gain.
- **prop-main-model-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-model-main-model-estimates)): With the weights and sums $S_{j,Z}(x)$ defined in Section <a href="#sec:weights" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:weights">4</a>, there is a fixed exponent $C_5$ such that $$\begin{aligned} \\|H_0(Z)S_{0,Z}(x)-A_x\\|_2&\ll k^{-1}, \label{model:first-piece-norm}\\ \\|H_j(Z)S_{j,Z}(x)\\|_2&\ll(k2^j)^{C_5}\delta_j^k \qquad(1\le j\le J). \label{model:later-piece-norm} \end{aligned}$$ These bounds are uniform in $x\in\mathcal X$ and over the entire range $k_0\le k\le\ell^{3/5}$, with one fixed $k_0$ and one sufficiently large $X$ threshold.
  - Explanation: In the independent model, the mollified first piece is close to the positive main term and the later pieces are geometrically small.
- **prop-tail-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-tail-count)): There are $k_0$, $X_0$, and $C$, depending on $E$, such that for every $F\in\mathcal F$, every dyadic $X\ge X_0$, and every integer $$k_0+1\le k\le(\log X)^{3/5},$$ one has $$\#\{d\in\mathcal A(X):a(F^{(d)})>k\}\le C Xk^{-2}.$$
  - Explanation: The number of admissible twists in a dyadic block with analytic rank above k is bounded by the block size over k squared throughout the allowed range.
- **lemma-jensen-pointwise-rank** (bound, [source](#stmt-tail-jensen)): Uniformly for $F\in\mathcal F$ and $d\in\mathcal A(X)$, $$a(F^{(d)})\ll_E\log(3X).$$
  - Explanation: Each individual analytic rank is at most a constant multiple of the logarithm of the height, uniformly over the family, via Jensen's formula.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-analytic-gauss",
        "exact_quote": "For fixed $h$, the function $l\\mapsto B_h(l)$ is multiplicative on odd positive integers, and $$\\label{analytic:phase}\n B_{-h}(l)=\\left(\\frac{-1}{l}\\right)B_h(l),\\qquad\n \\epsilon_l B_h(l)=\\frac{1+i}{2}B_h(l)+\\frac{1-i}{2}B_{-h}(l).$$ If $h\\ne0$, $p$ is odd, $t=v_p(h)$, and $v\\geq1$, then $$\\label{analytic:prime-powers}\n B_h(p^v)=\n \\begin{cases}\n p^{v/2}(1-p^{-1}),&v\\text{ even},\\ v\\leq t,\\\\\n -p^{v/2-1},&v\\text{ even},\\ v=t+1,\\\\\n p^{(v-1)/2}\\left(\\dfrac{h/p^{v-1}}p\\right),\n       &v\\text{ odd},\\ v=t+1,\\\\\n 0,&\\text{otherwise}.\n \\end{cases}$$ Consequently $B_h(p^v)=0$ for $v>t+1$ and $|B_h(p^v)|\\leq p^{v/2}$. If $t=0$, only $v=1$ can survive and $B_h(p)=(h/p)$. If $t=1$, only $v=2$ can survive and $B_h(p^2)=-1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-006.2"
      },
      "evidence": [],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-analytic-euler",
        "exact_quote": "Let $r$ be positive and odd, and write $h=h_1h_2^2\\ne0$, where $h_1$ is signed squarefree and $h_2\\geq1$. For $\\nu\\in\\{1,-1\\}$, the series $$\\mathcal G_{h,r}^{\\nu}(s_1,s_2)=\n \\sum_{\\substack{n_1,n_2\\geq1\\\\n_1,n_2\\ \\mathrm{odd}}}\n \\frac{\\lambda_F(n_1)\\lambda_F(n_2)B_{\\nu h}(rn_1n_2)}\n      {n_1^{s_1}n_2^{s_2}}$$ initially converges in a right half-plane and continues to $\\Re s_1,\\Re s_2>1/2$ as $$\\label{analytic:euler-factorization}\n \\mathcal G_{h,r}^{\\nu}(s_1,s_2)=\n L_{F^{(\\nu h_1)}}(s_1)L_{F^{(\\nu h_1)}}(s_2)\n \\mathcal Q_{h,r}^{\\nu}(s_1,s_2).$$ For $0<b\\leq1/4$ and $\\Re s_i\\geq1/2+b$, $$\\label{analytic:euler-bound}\n |\\mathcal Q_{h,r}^{\\nu}(s_1,s_2)|\n \\ll_{\\mathcal F} b^{-C_{\\rm ar}}r^{C_{\\rm ar}}\n                       \\tau(h_2)^{C_{\\rm ar}}.$$ The function $\\mathcal Q$ is holomorphic in these open half-planes.",
        "source_id": "PM-006.2"
      },
      "evidence": [],
      "exact_statement": "Let $r$ be positive and odd, and write $h=h_1h_2^2\\ne0$, where $h_1$ is signed squarefree and $h_2\\geq1$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-analytic-poisson",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U>0$, let $r$ be odd and positive, and let $N_i$ be bounded below by a fixed positive constant. Fix $0<b\\leq1/4$. In the expression $$\\label{analytic:poisson-average}\n \\frac1U\\sum_{m\\in\\mathbb Z}\n \\sum_{\\substack{n_1,n_2\\geq1\\\\n_1,n_2\\ \\mathrm{odd}}}\n \\frac{\\lambda_F(n_1)\\lambda_F(n_2)}\n {(n_1n_2)^{1/2}n_1^{it_1}n_2^{it_2}}\n \\left(\\frac m{rn_1n_2}\\right)\n W(n_1/N_1,n_2/N_2,m/U),$$ Poisson summation gives the zero frequency, namely the complete-residue average integrated in $x$, together with the nonzero frequencies. For every fixed $J_1>0$, a sufficiently large fixed seminorm order $D$ bounds the absolute contribution of frequency $h=h_1h_2^2\\ne0$ by $$\\begin{aligned}\n &C_{J_1,\\mathcal F}\\|W\\|_{D,*}\n (N_1N_2)^{-1/2+b}b^{-C_{\\rm ar}}r^{C_{\\rm ar}}\n \\tau(h_2)^{C_{\\rm ar}}\n \\left(1+\\frac{|h|U}{rN_1N_2}\\right)^{-J_1}\n \\nonumber\\\\[-2pt]\n &\\hspace{8mm}\\cdot\n \\sum_{\\nu=\\pm1}\\int_{\\mathbb R^2}\n \\frac{\\prod_{i=1}^2\n |L_{F^{(\\nu h_1)}}(1/2+b+i(t_i+v_i))|}\n {(1+|v_1|+|v_2|)^{J_1}}\\,dv_1\\,dv_2.\n \\label{analytic:nonzero-mode}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If the Fourier transform of $W$ in $x$ is supported in a fixed interval $[-C_W,C_W]$, all modes with $|h|>C_Wa_1^2rN_1N_2/U$ vanish exactly.",
        "source_id": "PM-006.2"
      },
      "evidence": [],

For every fixed $J_1>0$, a sufficiently large fixed seminorm order $D$ bounds the absolute contribution of frequency $h=h_1h_2^2\\ne0$ by $$\\begin{aligned}\n &C_{J_1,\\mathcal F}\\|W\\|_{D,*}\n (N_1N_2)^{-1/2+b}b^{-C_{\\rm ar}}r^{C_{\\rm ar}}\n \\tau(h_2)^{C_{\\rm ar}}\n \\left(1+\\frac{|h|U}{rN_1N_2}\\right)^{-J_1}\n \\nonumber\\\\[-2pt]\n &\\hspace{8mm}\\cdot\n \\sum_{\\nu=\\pm1}\\int_{\\mathbb R^2}\n \\frac{\\prod_{i=1}^2\n |L_{F^{(\\nu h_1)}}(1/2+b+i(t_i+v_i))|}\n {(1+|v_1|+|v_2|)^{J_1}}\\,dv_1\\,dv_2.\n \\label{analytic:nonzero-mode}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If the Fourier transform of $W$ in $x$ is supported in a fixed interval $[-C_W,C_W]$, all modes with $|h|>C_Wa_1^2rN_1N_2/U$ vanish exactly.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-analytic-frequency-sum",
        "exact_quote": "For fixed $B\\geq0$, $J_1>1$, and $Q>0$, $$\\label{analytic:dyadic-frequency-bound}\n \\sum_{T=1,2,4,\\ldots}T\\log^B(3T)(1+T/Q)^{-J_1}\n \\ll_{B,J_1}\n \\begin{cases}\n Q^{J_1},&0<Q<1,\\\\\n Q\\log^B(3Q),&Q\\geq1.\n \\end{cases}$$ In particular the first bound is at most a constant times $Q$.",
        "source_id": "PM-006.2"
      },
      "evidence": [],
      "exact_statement": "For fixed $B\\geq0$, $J_1>1$, and $Q>0$, $$\\label{analytic:dyadic-frequency-bound}\n \\sum_{T=1,2,4,\\ldots}T\\log^B(3T)(1+T/Q)^{-J_1}\n \\ll_{B,J_1}\n \\begin{cases}\n Q^{J_1},&0<Q<1,\\\\\n Q\\log^B(3Q),&Q\\geq1.\n \\end{cases}$$ In particular the first bound is at most a constant times $Q$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-analytic-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\ell=\\log X$, where $X$ is sufficiently large, and let $$c_1\\ell^{-1/10}\\leq\\Delta\\leq\\frac12$$ for a fixed $c_1>0$. There are fixed constants $\\eta,c_2>0$ for which the following holds. Let $$S_V(x)=\\sum_{n\\geq1}\\frac{\\lambda_F(n)V(n)}{\\sqrt n}\\,\n                         w_x(\\ell^{-1}\\log n),\\qquad\n H_V=\\sum_{r'\\leq R_0}h(r')V(r'),$$ where $V=z_m$ or $Z$. Suppose that $S_V$ has length at most $X^{1-\\Delta}$, that $$\\label{analytic:multiplier-conditions}\n 1\\leq R_0\\leq X^{\\eta\\Delta},\\qquad\n \\sum_{r'\\leq R_0}|h(r')|\\leq C_H R_0^2,$$ and that $w_x$ and a function $A(x)$ have bounds by a fixed power of $\\ell$ for all derivatives up to a sufficiently large fixed order. For $w_x$ these bounds are required after smooth dyadic localization in $n$, on the scaled variables $n/N$ and $x$ in a fixed neighborhood of $\\operatorname{supp}\\Psi$. They may in particular follow from bounds in $u=\\ell^{-1}\\log n\\geq0$ and $x$, with a smooth extension at $n=1$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\frac1X\\sum_{m\\in\\mathbb Z}\\Psi(m/X)\n       |H_{z_m}S_{z_m}(m/X)-A(m/X)|^2\n \\nonumber\\\\\n &\\hspace{8mm}=\n \\int_{\\mathbb R}\\Psi(x)\\,\n       \\mathbb E|H_ZS_Z(x)-A(x)|^2\\,dx\n       +O\\big(\\exp(-c_2\\Delta\\ell)\\big).\n \\label{analytic:model-comparison}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The constants and the sufficiently large $X$ threshold are uniform when the stated support, coefficient, and derivative bounds are uniform. They do not depend on $\\Delta$ or on an index parametrizing such weights. One may take $$\\label{analytic:comparison-constants}\n C'=C_{\\rm ar}+1=257,\\qquad\n \\eta=\\frac1{4(4+2C')},\\qquad c_2=\\frac12.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-006.2"
      },
      "evidence": [],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\ell=\\log X$, where $X$ is sufficiently large, and let $$c_1\\ell^{-1/10}\\leq\\Delta\\leq\\frac12$$ for a fixed $c_1>0$. Let $$S_V(x)=\\sum_{n\\geq1}\\frac{\\lambda_F(n)V(n)}{\\sqrt n}\\,\n                         w_x(\\ell^{-1}\\log n),\\qquad\n H_V=\\sum_{r'\\leq R_0}h(r')V(r'),$$ where $V=z_m$ or $Z$. Suppose that $S_V$ has length at most $X^{1-\\Delta}$, that $$\\label{analytic:multiplier-conditions}\n 1\\leq R_0\\leq X^{\\eta\\Delta},\\qquad\n \\sum_{r'\\leq R_0}|h(r')|\\leq C_H R_0^2,$$ and that $w_x$ and a function $A(x)$ have bounds by a fixed power of $\\ell$ for all derivatives up to a sufficiently large fixed order. For $w_x$ these bounds are required after smooth dyadic localization in $n$, on the scaled variables $n/N$ and $x$ in a fixed neighborhood of $\\operatorname{supp}\\Psi$. They may in particular follow from bounds in $u=\\ell^{-1}\\log n\\geq0$ and $x$, with a smooth extension at $n=1$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\frac1X\\sum_{m\\in\\mathbb Z}\\Psi(m/X)\n       |H_{z_m}S_{z_m}(m/X)-A(m/X)|^2\n \\nonumber\\\\\n &\\hspace{8mm}=\n \\int_{\\mathbb R}\\Psi(x)\\,\n       \\mathbb E|H_ZS_Z(x)-A(x)|^2\\,dx\n       +O\\big(\\exp(-c_2\\Delta\\ell)\\big).\n \\label{analytic:model-comparison}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The constants and the sufficiently large $X$ threshold are uniform when the stated support, coefficient, and derivative bounds are uniform. They do not depend on $\\Delta$ or on an index parametrizing such weights. One may take $$\\label{analytic:comparison-constants}\n C'=C_{\\rm ar}+1=257,\\qquad\n \\eta=\\frac1{4(4+2C')},\\qquad c_2=\\frac12.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-weights-vanishing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $d$ be admissible, $X\\le |d|<2X$, and $x=d/X$. Set $$g_d(z)=L_{F^{(d)}}(1/2+z)(C_F|d|)^z\\Gamma(1+z),\n \\qquad \\epsilon_d=\\operatorname{sign}(F^{(d)}).$$ Then $g_d$ is entire, $g_d(-z)=\\epsilon_d g_d(z)$, and $$\\begin{split}\n \\mathcal S_d\n &:=\n \\sum_{n\\ge1}\\frac{\\lambda(n)\\chi_d(n)}{\\sqrt n}\n                 W_{k,x}(\\ell^{-1}\\log n)\\\\\n &=\\frac{k!}{\\ell^k}\\frac{1}{2\\pi i}\n       \\int_{(2)}g_d(z)\\frac{dz}{z^{k+1}}.\n \\end{split}\n \\label{weights:completed-integral}$$ More precisely, $$\\bigl(1+\\epsilon_d(-1)^k\\bigr)\\mathcal S_d\n       =\\ell^{-k}g_d^{(k)}(0).\n \\label{weights:exact-derivative}$$ Consequently, if $a(F^{(d)})>k$ and $\\epsilon_d=(-1)^k$, then $$\\sum_{n\\ge1}\\frac{\\lambda(n)\\chi_d(n)}{\\sqrt n}\n                  W_{k,d/X}(\\ell^{-1}\\log n)=0.\n \\label{weights:zero}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-006.2"
      },
      "evidence": [],
      "exact_statement": "Let $d$ be admissible, $X\\le |d|<2X$, and $x=d/X$. Set $$g_d(z)=L_{F^{(d)}}(1/2+z)(C_F|d|)^z\\Gamma(1+z),\n \\qquad \\epsilon_d=\\operatorname{sign}(F^{(d)}).$$ Then $g_d$ is entire, $g_d(-z)=\\epsilon_d g_d(z)$, and $$\\begin{split}\n \\mathcal S_d\n &:=\n \\sum_{n\\ge1}\\frac{\\lambda(n)\\chi_d(n)}{\\sqrt n}\n                 W_{k,x}(\\ell^{-1}\\log n)\\\\\n &=\\frac{k!}{\\ell^k}\\frac{1}{2\\pi i}\n       \\int_{(2)}g_d(z)\\frac{dz}{z^{k+1}}.\n \\end{split}\n \\label{weights:completed-integral}$$ More precisely, $$\\bigl(1+\\epsilon_d(-1)^k\\bigr)\\mathcal S_d\n       =\\ell^{-k}g_d^{(k)}(0).\n \\label{weights:exact-derivative}$$ Consequently, if $a(F^{(d)})>k$ and $\\epsilon_d=(-1)^k$, then $$\\sum_{n\\ge1}\\frac{\\lambda(n)\\chi_d(n)}{\\sqrt n}\n                  W_{k,d/X}(\\ell^{-1}\\log n)=0.\n \\label{weights:zero}$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-weights-derivatives",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $r\\ge0$, and then take $k_0\\ge r+2$. For $a,b\\ge0$, $a+b\\le r$, uniformly in <a href=\"#weights:range\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"weights:range\">[weights:range]</a>, $x\\in\\mathcal K$, and the indicated arguments, one has $$\\begin{aligned}\n |\\partial_u^a\\partial_x^b w_{j,x}(u)|\n   &\\le C_r(k/\\delta_j)^r\\delta_j^k\n                  &&(1\\le j\\le J,\\ u\\in\\mathbb R), \\label{weights:short-derivatives}\\\\\n |\\partial_u^a\\partial_x^b w_{0,x}(u)|\n   &\\le C_r k^r\n                  &&(u\\ge-\\log2/\\ell). \\label{weights:main-derivatives}\n\\end{aligned}$$ There is also $c>0$, independent of $r,k,X$, such that, with $\\delta=\\delta_J$ and $n=e^{\\ell u}>0$, $$|\\partial_u^a\\partial_x^b w_{*,x}(u)|\n       \\le C_r(k/\\delta)^r\\delta^k e^{-cn/X}.\n \\label{weights:terminal-derivatives}$$ Changing the fixed compact neighborhood of $\\mathcal K$ changes only the constants.",
        "source_id": "PM-006.2"
      },
      "evidence": [],
      "exact_statement": "Fix an integer $r\\ge0$, and then take $k_0\\ge r+2$. They have length $X^{1-\\Delta_j}$, with $$\\Delta_j=\\delta_j/4,\\qquad\n \\tfrac14\\ell^{-1/10}\\le\\Delta_j\\le\\tfrac14 .$$ The deterministic term $$s=k/\\ell,\\qquad A_x=(C_F|x|)^s\\Gamma(1+s)\n \\label{weights:Ax}$$ has uniformly bounded derivatives of each fixed order on $\\mathcal K$, and is bounded above and below by positive constants.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-weights-terminal",
        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed exponent $C_T$, chosen before $k_0$, such that uniformly in <a href=\"#weights:range\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"weights:range\">[weights:range]</a>, $$\\frac1X\\sum_{\\substack{d\\ {\\rm admissible}\\\\X\\le|d|<2X}}\n       |S_{*,\\chi_d}(d/X)|^2\n       \\ll \\ell^{C_T}\\delta_J^{2k}.\n \\label{weights:terminal-square}$$ The same estimate holds after restricting the summation to either value of $\\chi_d(2)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-006.2"
      },
      "evidence": [],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-model-mollifier-length",
        "exact_quote": "The polynomial $H_j$ has an expansion $$H_j(V)=\\sum_{r\\le R_j}h_j(r)V(r),\\qquad\n |h_j(r)|\\le\\tau(r)r^{-1/2},\\qquad\n \\sum_r|h_j(r)|\\ll R_j^2,$$ where, for a fixed constant $C_{\\rm len}>0$, one may take $$R_j=\\exp\\left(C_{\\rm len}(k^{1/4}+j+1)\n                     \\ell\\delta_j^2/\\sqrt k\\right).\n \\label{model:length-bound}$$ For every fixed $\\eta>0$, a sufficiently large fixed $k_0$ ensures $R_j\\le X^{\\eta\\delta_j/4}$ for all $0\\le j\\le J$ and all $k$ in the stated range.         "source_id": "PM-006.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-model-good-event",
        "exact_quote": "Except for $O(Xk^{-2})$ integers in $X\\le|m|<2X$, the following inequalities hold simultaneously: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\frac12\\le H_j(z_m)P_j^{\\mathrm{eu}}(z_m)&\\le2\n                         &&(0\\le j\\le J),\n \\label{model:good-mollifier}\\\\\n \\frac{P_0^{\\mathrm{eu}}(z_m)}{P_j^{\\mathrm{eu}}(z_m)}\n   &\\ge2^{-jk/8} &&(1\\le j\\le J),\n \\label{model:good-ratio}\\\\\n P_0^{\\mathrm{eu}}(z_m)&\\ge2^{-Jk/8}.\n \\label{model:good-full-product}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The implied constant and the lower threshold $k_0$ do not depend on $k,j,X$.",
        "source_id": "PM-006.2"
      },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **analytic and algebraic rank** ([source](#sec:introduction)): We call $a$ the analytic rank and write $r(E^{(d)})=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb Z}E^{(d)}(\mathbb Q)$ for the algebraic or Mordell–Weil rank.
- **bad-prime set** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Fix the primes $$S=\{p:p\mid 2q_E\},\qquad Q_S=\prod_{p\in S}p.$$
- **finite family F** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Let $\mathcal F$ be the finite set, up to $\mathbb Q$-isomorphism, of curves obtained from $E$ by twisting by signed squarefree products of primes in $S$.
- **admissible parameter** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Call a signed squarefree integer $d$ *admissible* if $$d\equiv1\pmod4,\qquad (d,Q_S)=1.$$
- **dyadic admissible block** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For a dyadic $X$, put $$\mathcal A(X)=\{d:d\text{ admissible},\ X\le\|d\|<2X\}.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-mean-rank-limit | limiting average analytic rank over signed squarefree twists | = | $\frac12$ | limit as Y tends to infinity |
| q-density-rank-zero-one | density of twists of analytic rank j for j equal to zero or one | = | $\frac12$ | limit as Y tends to infinity, each of the two ranks |
| q-rank-tail-bound | normalized rank mass of twists with analytic rank above R | ≤ | $\frac{C_E}{R}$ | limsup as Y tends to infinity |
| q-algebraic-exponential-moment | limit of the exponential moment of algebraic rank | = | $\frac{1+e^t}{2}$ | fixed t, limit as Y tends to infinity |
| q-algebraic-power-moment | limit of the m-th power moment of algebraic rank | = | $\frac12$ | fixed m, limit as Y tends to infinity |
| q-arithmetic-exponent | fixed arithmetic exponent in the Euler correction bound | = | $256$ | fixed once for all later requirements |
| q-comparison-eta | allowed multiplier-length exponent in the model comparison | = | $\frac1{4(4+2C')}$ | fixed constant depending on C' |
| q-scale-width | width of the j-th dyadic scale | = | $2^{-j}$ | scale index j up to J |
| q-derivative-range | permitted range of the derivative order k | ≤ | $\lfloor\ell^{3/5}\rfloor$ | k at least the fixed base order and at most a fixed power of the logarithm of X |
| q-count-large-rank | number of admissible twists in a dyadic block with analytic rank above k | ≤ | $C Xk^{-2}$ | k between the base order plus one and a fixed power of the logarithm |
| q-pointwise-rank-bound | individual analytic rank of an admissible twist | ≤ | $\log(3X)$ | uniform over the family |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-reduction** [claim](#stmt-setup-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-setup-reduction): Factor out the part of the twist parameter supported on the bad primes, absorb it into the curve, and fix the sign to reach an admissible parameter. -> admissible reduction with bounded multiplicity
- **step-local-factors** [claim](#stmt-setup-local-factors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-setup-local-factors): Compute inertia-invariant Euler factors and the Artin conductor of the twisted Tate module representation. -> twisted Dirichlet series and conductor formula
- **step-gauss-sums** [claim](#stmt-analytic-gauss) / [proof](#proof-stmt-analytic-gauss): Chinese remainder theorem and quadratic reciprocity for multiplicativity, then explicit prime-power Gauss sum evaluation. -> multiplicativity and prime-power formulas
- **step-euler-factorization** [claim](#stmt-analytic-euler) / [proof](#proof-stmt-analytic-euler): Multiplicativity gives local sums; compare with inverse local factors of the twisted curve and bound the correction prime by prime. -> factorization with bounded Euler correction
- **step-poisson** [claim](#stmt-analytic-poisson) / [proof](#proof-stmt-analytic-poisson): Poisson summation in residue classes, Mellin inversion and contour shifts using the Euler factorization. -> zero-frequency model term plus bounded nonzero frequencies
- **step-frequency-sum** [claim](#stmt-analytic-frequency-sum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-analytic-frequency-sum): Split the dyadic sum at the scale parameter and sum two geometric series. -> two-regime bound for the frequency sum
- **step-log-moments** [claim](#stmt-analytic-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-analytic-poisson-2): Simultaneous induction on the height using prime-square inflation, Poisson summation, an approximate functional equation and a strip principle. -> logarithmic second moments of twisted L-functions
- **step-polynomial-moments** [claim](#stmt-analytic-polynomial-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-analytic-poisson-2): Repeat the polynomial part of the induction for an arbitrary fixed smooth weight after the L-function bound is established. -> second moments of localized Dirichlet polynomials
- **step-model-comparison** [claim](#stmt-analytic-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-analytic-comparison): Expand the square, apply the Poisson estimate to each localized term, identify the zero frequency with the model, and sum dual frequencies. -> integer averages equal model averages up to exponentially small error
- **step-completed-derivative** [claim](#stmt-weights-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-weights-vanishing): Mellin inversion for the truncated power and gamma integral, then a contour shift across the pole at zero and the reflection law. -> smoothed sum equals a central derivative, vanishing for large rank with matching sign
- **step-weight-derivatives** [claim](#stmt-weights-derivatives) / [proof](#proof-stmt-weights-derivatives): Differentiate under the integral and bound the integrand with an elementary exponential inequality and a gamma integral. -> fixed-order derivative bounds
- **step-comparison-interface** [claim](#stmt-weights-comparison-interface) / [proof](#proof-stmt-weights-comparison-interface): Check polynomial derivative bounds in the log variable and extend the first weight smoothly past the endpoint. -> weights satisfy the comparison hypotheses
- **step-terminal-square** [claim](#stmt-weights-terminal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-weights-terminal): Dyadic partition, Mellin transform of the terminal weight, Minkowski inequality and the polynomial second moment. -> terminal mean-square bound
- **step-terminal-absorption** [claim](#stmt-weights-terminal-small) / [proof](#proof-stmt-weights-terminal-small): Enlarge the base derivative order so the loss is dominated by the geometric factor. -> geometrically small terminal term and absorbed comparison errors
- **step-mollifier-length** [claim](#stmt-model-mollifier-length) / [proof](#proof-stmt-model-mollifier-length): Count monomials in the truncated prime blocks and bound the coefficients by the divisor function. -> length and coefficient bounds
- **step-prime-sum-moments** [claim](#stmt-model-integer-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-model-integer-moments): Expand the moment in the model, group by partitions of positions, and pass to integers by periodicity. -> moment bound for prime sums
- **step-good-event** [claim](#stmt-model-good-event) / [proof](#proof-stmt-model-good-event): Cauchy coefficient estimates on block polynomials and Chebyshev inequality using the prime-sum moments. -> simultaneous product event outside a small set
- **step-exact-shifts** [claim](#stmt-model-exact-shifts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-model-exact-shifts): Compute local second moments of ratios of Euler factors, multiply by independence and sum over primes with elementary prime estimates. -> norm bounds for shifted exact products
- **step-relative-truncation** [claim](#stmt-model-relative-truncation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-model-relative-truncation): Cauchy coefficient formula blockwise and telescoping of relative errors across independent blocks. -> relative mean-square truncation error
- **step-shifted-norms** [claim](#stmt-model-shifted-norms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-model-shifted-norms): Specialize the exact-product bound at three shifts and transfer to the mollifiers by relative truncation. -> mollified norm bounds
- **step-global-power** [claim](#stmt-model-global-power-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-model-global-power-approximation): Compare logarithms of the derivative to the exponential, using a maximum argument for large arguments and Taylor expansion for small ones. -> uniform derivative error bound
- **step-first-fourier** [claim](#stmt-model-first-fourier-error) / [proof](#proof-stmt-model-first-fourier-error): Differentiate the gamma-integral representation, use the power approximation, and extend and integrate by parts. -> rapid Fourier decay with a one-over-k gain
- **step-main-model** [claim](#stmt-model-main-model-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-model-main-model-estimates): Fourier inversion into finite Euler products, then Minkowski inequality with the shifted-norm bounds and Fourier decay. -> model mean-square estimates for the weight pieces
- **step-tail-count** [claim](#stmt-tail-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tail-count): Transfer model estimates to integers, apply Chebyshev, intersect with the product event, show the weighted sum is nonzero, and contradict the vanishing identity; handle the other sign with the previous order. -> counting bound for large rank
- **step-jensen** [claim](#stmt-tail-jensen) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tail-jensen): Bound the L-function on a strip using the functional equation and a strip principle, then apply Jensen's formula on a disk around the center. -> pointwise logarithmic rank bound
- **step-tail-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tail-jensen-2): Sum integer tails of the counting bound up to the logarithmic range, bound the remainder with the pointwise bound, cover by dyadic blocks and pass back to arbitrary twists. -> rank-weighted tail bound
- **step-mean-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-mean) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tail-jensen-3): Use the squarefree count, the companion densities for ranks zero and one, the tail bound for the middle and high ranks, and let the threshold tend to infinity. -> mean analytic rank
- **step-algebraic-moments** [claim](#stmt-cor-algebraic-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tail-jensen-4): Combine density-one equality of ranks with an external exponential-moment bound via Cauchy–Schwarz on the exceptional set. -> limits of algebraic-rank moments

          "exact_quote": "For every $F\\in\\mathcal F$ and signed squarefree $b$, there are $F'\\in\\mathcal F$ and an admissible $d$ such that $$F^{(b)}\\simeq (F')^{(d)},\\qquad |d|\\le |b|.$$ The correspondence can be chosen to have bounded multiplicity, uniformly in $b$. Every sum of nonnegative functions of these twists can therefore be bounded by a fixed multiple of the corresponding sums over $\\mathcal F$ and admissible parameters of no greater height.",
          "source_id": "PM-006.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-reduction",
      "inputs": [
        "finite family of twists",
        "signed squarefree parameter"
      ],
      "method": "Factor out the part of the twist parameter supported on the bad primes, absorb it into the curve, and fix the sign to reach an admissible parameter.",
      "output": "admissible reduction with bounded multiplicity",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-setup-reduction"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-setup-local-factors",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-setup-local-factors",
          "exact_quote": "For $F\\in\\mathcal F$ and admissible $d$, $$\\label{eq:setup-twist}\n L_{F^{(d)}}(s)=\\sum_{n\\ge1}\\lambda_F(n)\\chi_d(n)n^{-s},\n \\qquad q_{F^{(d)}}=q_F|d|^2,\n \\qquad \\chi_d(n)=\\left(\\frac dn\\right).$$ Here the symbol is the Kronecker symbol and $d=1$ has the untwisted convention. For an arbitrary signed squarefree $b$, the same good-prime twisting rule holds at primes outside $S$: the local trace is multiplied by $(b/p)$ when $p\\nmid b$, and the local factor is one when $p\\mid b$.",
          "source_id": "PM-006.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-local-factors",
      "inputs": [
        "admissible parameter"
      ],
      "method": "Compute inertia-invariant Euler factors and the Artin conductor of the twisted Tate module representation.",
      "output": "twisted Dirichlet series and conductor formula",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-setup-local-factors"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-analytic-gauss",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-analytic-gauss",
          "exact_quote": "For fixed $h$, the function $l\\mapsto B_h(l)$ is multiplicative on odd positive integers, and $$\\label{analytic:phase}\n B_{-h}(l)=\\left(\\frac{-1}{l}\\right)B_h(l),\\qquad\n \\epsilon_l B_h(l)=\\frac{1+i}{2}B_h(l)+\\frac{1-i}{2}B_{-h}(l).$$ If $h\\ne0$, $p$ is odd, $t=v_p(h)$, and $v\\geq1$, then $$\\label{analytic:prime-powers}\n B_h(p^v)=\n \\begin{cases}\n p^{v/2}(1-p^{-1}),&v\\text{ even},\\ v\\leq t,\\\\\n -p^{v/2-1},&v\\text{ even},\\ v=t+1,\\\\\n p^{(v-1)/2}\\left(\\dfrac{h/p^{v-1}}p\\right),\n       &v\\text{ odd},\\ v=t+1,\\\\\n 0,&\\text{otherwise}.\n \\end{cases}$$ Consequently $B_h(p^v)=0$ for $v>t+1$ and $|B_h(p^v)|\\leq p^{v/2}$. If $t=0$, only $v=1$ can survive and $B_h(p)=(h/p)$. If $t=1$, only $v=2$ can survive and $B_h(p^2)=-1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-006.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-gauss-sums",
      "inputs": [
        "normalized Gauss sums"
      ],
      "method": "Chinese remainder theorem and quadratic reciprocity for multiplicativity, then explicit prime-power Gauss sum evaluation.",
      "output": "multiplicativity and prime-power formulas",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-analytic-gauss"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-analytic-euler",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-gauss-sums"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-analytic-euler",
          "exact_quote": "Let $r$ be positive and odd, and write $h=h_1h_2^2\\ne0$, where $h_1$ is signed squarefree and $h_2\\geq1$. For $\\nu\\in\\{1,-1\\}$, the series $$\\mathcal G_{h,r}^{\\nu}(s_1,s_2)=\n \\sum_{\\substack{n_1,n_2\\geq1\\\\n_1,n_2\\ \\mathrm{odd}}}\n \\frac{\\lambda_F(n_1)\\lambda_F(n_2)B_{\\nu h}(rn_1n_2)}\n      {n_1^{s_1}n_2^{s_2}}$$ initially converges in a right half-plane and continues to $\\Re s_1,\\Re s_2>1/2$ as $$\\label{analytic:euler-factorization}\n \\mathcal G_{h,r}^{\\nu}(s_1,s_2)=\n L_{F^{(\\nu h_1)}}(s_1)L_{F^{(\\nu h_1)}}(s_2)\n \\mathcal Q_{h,r}^{\\nu}(s_1,s_2).$$ For $0<b\\leq1/4$ and $\\Re s_i\\geq1/2+b$, $$\\label{analytic:euler-bound}\n |\\mathcal Q_{h,r}^{\\nu}(s_1,s_2)|\n \\ll_{\\mathcal F} b^{-C_{\\rm ar}}r^{C_{\\rm ar}}\n                       \\tau(h_2)^{C_{\\rm ar}}.$$ The function $\\mathcal Q$ is holomorphic in these open half-planes.",
          "source_id": "PM-006.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-euler-factorization",
      "inputs": [
        "Gauss-sum formulas"
      ],
      "method": "Multiplicativity gives local sums; compare with inverse local factors of the twisted curve and bound the correction prime by prime.",
      "output": "factorization with bounded Euler correction",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-analytic-euler"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-analytic-poisson",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-gauss-sums",
        "step-euler-factorization"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-analytic-poisson",
          "exact_quote": "Let $U>0$, let $r$ be odd and positive, and let $N_i$ be bounded below by a fixed positive constant. Fix $0<b\\leq1/4$. In the expression $$\\label{analytic:poisson-average}\n \\frac1U\\sum_{m\\in\\mathbb Z}\n \\sum_{\\substack{n_1,n_2\\geq1\\\\n_1,n_2\\ \\mathrm{odd}}}\n \\frac{\\lambda_F(n_1)\\lambda_F(n_2)}\n {(n_1n_2)^{1/2}n_1^{it_1}n_2^{it_2}}\n \\left(\\frac m{rn_1n_2}\\right)\n W(n_1/N_1,n_2/N_2,m/U),$$ Poisson summation gives the zero frequency, namely the complete-residue average integrated in $x$, together with the nonzero frequencies. For every fixed $J_1>0$, a sufficiently large fixed seminorm order $D$ bounds the absolute contribution of frequency $h=h_1h_2^2\\ne0$ by $$\\begin{aligned}\n &C_{J_1,\\mathcal F}\\|W\\|_{D,*}\n (N_1N_2)^{-1/2+b}b^{-C_{\\rm ar}}r^{C_{\\rm ar}}\n \\tau(h_2)^{C_{\\rm ar}}\n \\left(1+\\frac{|h|U}{rN_1N_2}\\right)^{-J_1}\n \\nonumber\\\\[-2pt]\n &\\hspace{8mm}\\cdot\n \\sum_{\\nu=\\pm1}\\int_{\\mathbb R^2}\n \\frac{\\prod_{i=1}^2\n |L_{F^{(\\nu h_1)}}(1/2+b+i(t_i+v_i))|}\n {(1+|v_1|+|v_2|)^{J_1}}\\,dv_1\\,dv_2.\n \\label{analytic:nonzero-mode}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If the Fourier transform of $W$ in $x$ is supported in a fixed interval $[-C_W,C_W]$, all modes with $|h|>C_Wa_1^2rN_1N_2/U$ vanish exactly.",
          "source_id": "PM-006.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-poisson",
      "inputs": [
        "Euler factorization",
        "Gauss sums"
      ],
      "method": "Poisson summation in residue classes, Mellin inversion and contour shifts using the Euler factorization.",
      "output": "zero-frequency model term plus bounded nonzero frequencies",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-analytic-poisson"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-analytic-frequency-sum",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-analytic-frequency-sum",
          "exact_quote": "For fixed $B\\geq0$, $J_1>1$, and $Q>0$, $$\\label{analytic:dyadic-frequency-bound}\n \\sum_{T=1,2,4,\\ldots}T\\log^B(3T)(1+T/Q)^{-J_1}\n \\ll_{B,J_1}\n \\begin{cases}\n Q^{J_1},&0<Q<1,\\\\\n Q\\log^B(3Q),&Q\\geq1.\n \\end{cases}$$ In particular the first bound is at most a constant times $Q$.",
          "source_id": "PM-006.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-frequency-sum",
      "inputs": [
        "dyadic frequency weights"
      ],
      "method": "Split the dyadic sum at the scale parameter and sum two geometric series.",
      "output": "two-regime bound for the frequency sum",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-analytic-frequency-sum"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-analytic-moment",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-poisson",
        "step-reduction"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-analytic-moment",
          "exact_quote": "There are fixed positive integers $A,B$ such that, for every dyadic $M\\geq1$, every $F\\in\\mathcal F$, every $t\\in\\mathbb R$, and $1/2\\leq\\sigma\\leq2$, $$\\label{analytic:L-moment}\n \\frac1M\\sum_{\\substack{d\\ \\mathrm{admissible}\\\\M\\leq|d|<2M}}\n |L_{F^{(d)}}(\\sigma+it)|^2\n \\ll_{\\mathcal F}\\log^B(3M)(1+|t|)^A.$$ For arbitrary signed squarefree twisting parameters and $H\\geq1$, $$\\label{analytic:prefix-moment}\n \\sum_{\\substack{0<|h|\\leq H\\\\h\\ \\mathrm{squarefree}}}\n |L_{F^{(h)}}(\\sigma+it)|^2\n \\ll_{\\mathcal F}H\\log^B(3H)(1+|t|)^A.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-006.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-log-moments",
      "inputs": [
        "Poisson estimate",
        "reduction lemma"
      ],
      "method": "Simultaneous induction on the height using prime-square inflation, Poisson summation, an approximate functional equation and a strip principle.",
      "output": "logarithmic second moments of twisted L-functions",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-analytic-poisson-2"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-analytic-polynomial-moment",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-log-moments"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-analytic-polynomial-moment",
          "exact_quote": "With the same fixed integers $A,B$, every fixed smooth $G$ of compact support in $(0,\\infty)$ satisfies $$\\label{analytic:polynomial-bound}\n \\frac1M\\sum_{\\substack{d\\ \\mathrm{admissible}\\\\M\\leq|d|<2M}}\n |P_d(N,t;G)|^2\n \\ll_{\\mathcal F,G}\n \\log^B(3M)(1+|t|)^A(1+N/M)^A\n \\qquad(N>0).$$ The bound is uniform for fixed uniformly smooth families of weights with common compact support.",
          "source_id": "PM-006.2"
        }

      "id": "step-polynomial-moments",
      "inputs": [

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-analytic-poisson-2"

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-analytic-comparison",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-poisson",
        "step-frequency-sum",
        "step-log-moments"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-analytic-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\ell=\\log X$, where $X$ is sufficiently large, and let $$c_1\\ell^{-1/10}\\leq\\Delta\\leq\\frac12$$ for a fixed $c_1>0$. There are fixed constants $\\eta,c_2>0$ for which the following holds. Let $$S_V(x)=\\sum_{n\\geq1}\\frac{\\lambda_F(n)V(n)}{\\sqrt n}\\,\n                         w_x(\\ell^{-1}\\log n),\\qquad\n H_V=\\sum_{r'\\leq R_0}h(r')V(r'),$$ where $V=z_m$ or $Z$. Suppose that $S_V$ has length at most $X^{1-\\Delta}$, that $$\\label{analytic:multiplier-conditions}\n 1\\leq R_0\\leq X^{\\eta\\Delta},\\qquad\n \\sum_{r'\\leq R_0}|h(r')|\\leq C_H R_0^2,$$ and that $w_x$ and a function $A(x)$ have bounds by a fixed power of $\\ell$ for all derivatives up to a sufficiently large fixed order. For $w_x$ these bounds are required after smooth dyadic localization in $n$, on the scaled variables $n/N$ and $x$ in a fixed neighborhood of $\\operatorname{supp}\\Psi$. They may in particular follow from bounds in $u=\\ell^{-1}\\log n\\geq0$ and $x$, with a smooth extension at $n=1$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\frac1X\\sum_{m\\in\\mathbb Z}\\Psi(m/X)\n       |H_{z_m}S_{z_m}(m/X)-A(m/X)|^2\n \\nonumber\\\\\n &\\hspace{8mm}=\n \\int_{\\mathbb R}\\Psi(x)\\,\n       \\mathbb E|H_ZS_Z(x)-A(x)|^2\\,dx\n       +O\\big(\\exp(-c_2\\Delta\\ell)\\big).\n \\label{analytic:model-comparison}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The constants and the sufficiently large $X$ threshold are uniform when the stated support, coefficient, and derivative bounds are uniform. They do not depend on $\\Delta$ or on an index parametrizing such weights. One may take $$\\label{analytic:comparison-constants}\n C'=C_{\\rm ar}+1=257,\\qquad\n \\eta=\\frac1{4(4+2C')},\\qquad c_2=\\frac12.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-006.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-model-comparison",
      "inputs": [
        "Poisson estimate",
        "frequency sum lemma",
        "second moments"
      ],
      "method": "Expand the square, apply the Poisson estimate to each localized term, identify the zero frequency with the model, and sum dual frequencies.",
      "output": "integer averages equal model averages up to exponentially small error",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-analytic-comparison"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-weights-vanishing",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-weights-vanishing",
          "exact_quote": "Let $d$ be admissible, $X\\le |d|<2X$, and $x=d/X$. Set $$g_d(z)=L_{F^{(d)}}(1/2+z)(C_F|d|)^z\\Gamma(1+z),\n \\qquad \\epsilon_d=\\operatorname{sign}(F^{(d)}).$$ Then $g_d$ is entire, $g_d(-z)=\\epsilon_d g_d(z)$, and $$\\begin{split}\n \\mathcal S_d\n &:=\n \\sum_{n\\ge1}\\frac{\\lambda(n)\\chi_d(n)}{\\sqrt n}\n                 W_{k,x}(\\ell^{-1}\\log n)\\\\\n &=\\frac{k!}{\\ell^k}\\frac{1}{2\\pi i}\n       \\int_{(2)}g_d(z)\\frac{dz}{z^{k+1}}.\n \\end{split}\n \\label{weights:completed-integral}$$ More precisely, $$\\bigl(1+\\epsilon_d(-1)^k\\bigr)\\mathcal S_d\n       =\\ell^{-k}g_d^{(k)}(0).\n \\label{weights:exact-derivative}$$ Consequently, if $a(F^{(d)})>k$ and $\\epsilon_d=(-1)^k$, then $$\\sum_{n\\ge1}\\frac{\\lambda(n)\\chi_d(n)}{\\sqrt n}\n                  W_{k,d/X}(\\ell^{-1}\\log n)=0.\n \\label{weights:zero}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-006.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-completed-derivative",
      "inputs": [
        "completed L-function functional equation"
      ],
      "method": "Mellin inversion for the truncated power and gamma integral, then a contour shift across the pole at zero and the reflection law.",
      "output": "smoothed sum equals a central derivative, vanishing for large rank with matching sign",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-weights-vanishing"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-weights-derivatives",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-completed-derivative"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-weights-derivatives",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $r\\ge0$, and then take $k_0\\ge r+2$. For $a,b\\ge0$, $a+b\\le r$, uniformly in <a href=\"#weights:range\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"weights:range\">[weights:range]</a>, $x\\in\\mathcal K$, and the indicated arguments, one has $$\\begin{aligned}\n |\\partial_u^a\\partial_x^b w_{j,x}(u)|\n   &\\le C_r(k/\\delta_j)^r\\delta_j^k\n                  &&(1\\le j\\le J,\\ u\\in\\mathbb R), \\label{weights:short-derivatives}\\\\\n |\\partial_u^a\\partial_x^b w_{0,x}(u)|\n   &\\le C_r k^r\n                  &&(u\\ge-\\log2/\\ell). \\label{weights:main-derivatives}\n\\end{aligned}$$ There is also $c>0$, independent of $r,k,X$, such that, with $\\delta=\\delta_J$ and $n=e^{\\ell u}>0$, $$|\\partial_u^a\\partial_x^b w_{*,x}(u)|\n       \\le C_r(k/\\delta)^r\\delta^k e^{-cn/X}.\n \\label{weights:terminal-derivatives}$$ Changing the fixed compact neighborhood of $\\mathcal K$ changes only the constants.",
          "source_id": "PM-006.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-weight-derivatives",
      "inputs": [
        "weight definition and partition"
      ],
      "method": "Differentiate under the integral and bound the integrand with an elementary exponential inequality and a gamma integral.",
      "output": "fixed-order derivative bounds",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-weights-derivatives"
    },

          "anchor": "stmt-weights-comparison-interface",

They have length $X^{1-\\Delta_j}$, with $$\\Delta_j=\\delta_j/4,\\qquad\n \\tfrac14\\ell^{-1/10}\\le\\Delta_j\\le\\tfrac14 .$$ The deterministic term $$s=k/\\ell,\\qquad A_x=(C_F|x|)^s\\Gamma(1+s)\n \\label{weights:Ax}$$ has uniformly bounded derivatives of each fixed order on $\\mathcal K$, and is bounded above and below by positive constants. The endpoint enlargement at $n=1$ can be made inside the fixed range $n\\ge1/2$.",

          "anchor": "stmt-weights-terminal",

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-weights-terminal-small",

Moreover, for any fixed $c,L>0$, the comparison errors obey $$e^{-c\\Delta_j\\ell}\n       \\le k^{-L}2^{-Ljk}\\qquad(0\\le j\\le J)\n \\label{weights:comparison-absorption}$$ for one sufficiently large lower bound on $X$.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-model-mollifier-length",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-model-mollifier-length",
          "exact_quote": "The polynomial $H_j$ has an expansion $$H_j(V)=\\sum_{r\\le R_j}h_j(r)V(r),\\qquad\n |h_j(r)|\\le\\tau(r)r^{-1/2},\\qquad\n \\sum_r|h_j(r)|\\ll R_j^2,$$ where, for a fixed constant $C_{\\rm len}>0$, one may take $$R_j=\\exp\\left(C_{\\rm len}(k^{1/4}+j+1)\n                     \\ell\\delta_j^2/\\sqrt k\\right).\n \\label{model:length-bound}$$ For every fixed $\\eta>0$, a sufficiently large fixed $k_0$ ensures $R_j\\le X^{\\eta\\delta_j/4}$ for all $0\\le j\\le J$ and all $k$ in the stated range. Thus $H_j$ satisfies the multiplier hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#analytic:comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"analytic:comparison\">12</a>, with $\\Delta=\\delta_j/4$.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-model-integer-moments",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-model-integer-moments",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal I$ be any set of primes at most $Y_0$, and put $$B_{\\mathcal I}(V)=\\sum_{p\\in\\mathcal I}\n                 \\lambda(p)V(p)p^{-1/2-s}.$$ If $k_0$ is a sufficiently large absolute constant, then $$\\frac1X\\sum_{X\\le|m|<2X}|B_{\\mathcal I}(z_m)|^{64}\n \\ll\\left(1+\\sum_{p\\in\\mathcal I}\\frac1p\\right)^{32}\n \\label{model:64th-moment}$$ uniformly in $\\mathcal I,k,X$.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-model-good-event",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-prime-sum-moments",
        "step-mollifier-length"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-model-good-event",
          "exact_quote": "Except for $O(Xk^{-2})$ integers in $X\\le|m|<2X$, the following inequalities hold simultaneously: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\frac12\\le H_j(z_m)P_j^{\\mathrm{eu}}(z_m)&\\le2\n                         &&(0\\le j\\le J),\n \\label{model:good-mollifier}\\\\\n \\frac{P_0^{\\mathrm{eu}}(z_m)}{P_j^{\\mathrm{eu}}(z_m)}\n   &\\ge2^{-jk/8} &&(1\\le j\\le J),\n \\label{model:good-ratio}\\\\\n P_0^{\\mathrm{eu}}(z_m)&\\ge2^{-Jk/8}.\n \\label{model:good-full-product}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The implied constant and the lower threshold $k_0$ do not depend on $k,j,X$.",
          "source_id": "PM-006.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-good-event",
      "inputs": [
        "prime-sum moments",

      "method": "Cauchy coefficient estimates on block polynomials and Chebyshev inequality using the prime-sum moments.",
      "output": "simultaneous product event outside a small set",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-model-good-event"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-model-exact-shifts",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-model-exact-shifts",
          "exact_quote": "Let $0<s,\\alpha\\le1$, $b=\\alpha+it$, and $Y_j\\ge2$. If $h=\\alpha+s+|t|$ and $a=\\min(\\alpha,s)$, then $$\\|(P_j^{\\mathrm{eu}})^{-1}\\mathcal L_Q(b)\\|_2\n \\ll (1+h/a)^C\n       \\left(1+\\frac1{\\alpha\\log Y_j}\\right)^C.\n \\label{model:general-exact-norm}$$ At the real point $b=s$, with $j=0$ and the parameters of this section, $$\\|(P_0^{\\mathrm{eu}})^{-1}\\mathcal L_Q(s)-1\\|_2^2\n \\ll \\frac{e^{-2\\sqrt k}}{\\sqrt k}.\n \\label{model:exact-distance}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-006.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-exact-shifts",
      "inputs": [
        "local factor bounds"
      ],
      "method": "Compute local second moments of ratios of Euler factors, multiply by independence and sum over primes with elementary prime estimates.",
      "output": "norm bounds for shifted exact products",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-model-exact-shifts"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-model-relative-truncation",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-exact-shifts"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-model-relative-truncation",
          "exact_quote": "For every $b$ with $\\Re b>0$, uniformly in $Q\\ge Y_0$, $$\\|(H_j-(P_j^{\\mathrm{eu}})^{-1})\\mathcal L_Q(b)\\|_2\n \\le \\left(\\exp\\left(C\\sum_i2^{-m_{ji}}\\right)-1\\right)\n       \\|(P_j^{\\mathrm{eu}})^{-1}\\mathcal L_Q(b)\\|_2.\n \\label{model:relative-error}$$ In particular the relative factor is $O(2^{-k^{1/4}-j})$.",
          "source_id": "PM-006.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-relative-truncation",
      "inputs": [
        "exact shifted products"
      ],
      "method": "Cauchy coefficient formula blockwise and telescoping of relative errors across independent blocks.",
      "output": "relative mean-square truncation error",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-model-relative-truncation"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-model-shifted-norms",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-exact-shifts",
        "step-relative-truncation"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-model-shifted-norms",
          "exact_quote": "There are fixed $C,c_0>0$ such that, uniformly for every real $\\theta$ and every cutoff $Q\\ge Y_0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|H_0(Z)\\mathcal L_Q(s/2+i\\theta s)\\|_2\n       &\\ll(1+|\\theta|)^C,\n \\label{model:half-shift}\\\\\n \\|H_0(Z)\\mathcal L_Q(s)-1\\|_2\n       &\\ll e^{-c_0 k^{1/4}},\n \\label{model:mollified-distance}\\\\\n \\|H_j(Z)\\mathcal L_Q((1+i\\theta)/\\ell)\\|_2\n       &\\ll (1+|\\theta|)^C(k2^j)^C\n          \\qquad(0\\le j\\le J).\n \\label{model:unit-shift}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-006.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-shifted-norms",
      "inputs": [
        "exact shifted products",
        "relative truncation"
      ],
      "method": "Specialize the exact-product bound at three shifts and transfer to the mollifiers by relative truncation.",
      "output": "mollified norm bounds",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-model-shifted-norms"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-model-global-power-approximation",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-model-global-power-approximation",
          "exact_quote": "For each fixed nonnegative integer $r$, if $k\\ge2r+4$, then for every $z\\in\\mathbb R$, $$\\left|\\frac{d^r}{dz^r}\n           \\left((1-z/k)_+^k-e^{-z}\\right)\\right|\n \\le\\frac{C_r}{k}(1+|z|)^2e^{-z}.\n \\label{model:global-power-bound}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-006.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-global-power",
      "inputs": [
        "truncated power"
      ],
      "method": "Compare logarithms of the derivative to the exponential, using a maximum argument for large arguments and Taylor expansion for small ones.",
      "output": "uniform derivative error bound",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-model-global-power-approximation"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-model-first-fourier-error",

        "step-global-power"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-model-first-fourier-error",
          "exact_quote": "Define $$A_x=(C_F|x|)^s\\Gamma(1+s),\\qquad\n E_x(y)=w_{0,x}(y/k)-A_xe^{-y}\\quad(y\\ge0).$$ For every required fixed integer $r$, there is an extension $g_x$ of $e^{y/2}E_x(y)$ from $y\\ge0$ to $\\mathbb R$ such that $$|\\widehat g_x(\\theta)|\\ll_r k^{-1}(1+|\\theta|)^{-r}.\n \\label{model:first-Fourier-decay}$$ The constants are uniform in $x,k,X$ after choosing $k_0$ depending on $r$. The quantities $A_x$ are bounded above and below by positive fixed constants.",
          "source_id": "PM-006.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-first-fourier",
      "inputs": [
        "power approximation"
      ],
      "method": "Differentiate the gamma-integral representation, use the power approximation, and extend and integrate by parts.",
      "output": "rapid Fourier decay with a one-over-k gain",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-model-first-fourier-error"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-model-main-model-estimates",
      "depends_on": [

        "step-first-fourier",

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-model-main-model-estimates",
          "exact_quote": "With the weights and sums $S_{j,Z}(x)$ defined in Section <a href=\"#sec:weights\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:weights\">4</a>, there is a fixed exponent $C_5$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|H_0(Z)S_{0,Z}(x)-A_x\\|_2&\\ll k^{-1},\n \\label{model:first-piece-norm}\\\\\n \\|H_j(Z)S_{j,Z}(x)\\|_2&\\ll(k2^j)^{C_5}\\delta_j^k\n                    \\qquad(1\\le j\\le J).\n \\label{model:later-piece-norm}\n\\end{aligned}$$ These bounds are uniform in $x\\in\\mathcal X$ and over the entire range $k_0\\le k\\le\\ell^{3/5}$, with one fixed $k_0$ and one sufficiently large $X$ threshold.",

    },

          "source_id": "PM-006.2"

      "output": "counting bound for large rank",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-tail-count"
    },

          "exact_quote": "Uniformly for $F\\in\\mathcal F$ and $d\\in\\mathcal A(X)$, $$a(F^{(d)})\\ll_E\\log(3X).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-006.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-jensen",
      "inputs": [
        "functional equation",
        "lower bound at a point of absolute convergence"
      ],
      "method": "Bound the L-function on a strip using the functional equation and a strip principle, then apply Jensen's formula on a disk around the center.",
      "output": "pointwise logarithmic rank bound",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-tail-jensen"
    },
    {

          "source_id": "PM-006.2"
        }
      ],

        "pointwise rank bound",
        "reduction"

        }

For every fixed real $t$, $$\\lim_{Y\\to\\infty}\\frac1{\\#\\mathcal D(Y)}\n \\sum_{d\\in\\mathcal D(Y)}\\exp\\!\\bigl(t\\,r(E^{(d)})\\bigr)\n =\\frac{1+e^t}{2}.$$ For every fixed positive integer $m$, $$\\lim_{Y\\to\\infty}\\frac1{\\#\\mathcal D(Y)}\n \\sum_{d\\in\\mathcal D(Y)}r(E^{(d)})^m=\\frac12.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-006.2"

      "output": "limits of algebraic-rank moments",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-mean-half**: For each elliptic curve over the rationals, the average analytic rank over the signed squarefree twist parameters of absolute value at most Y tends to one half as Y tends to infinity.
- **inv-rank-tail**: The normalized rank mass carried by twists of analytic rank above a threshold R has limsup at most a constant divided by R, for every sufficiently large integer threshold.
- **inv-dyadic-count**: In each dyadic window of admissible parameters, the number of twists of analytic rank exceeding k is bounded by a constant times X divided by k squared, throughout the stated range of k.
- **inv-pointwise-jensen**: Every individual analytic rank in the admissible dyadic window is bounded by a constant multiple of log(3X).

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-individual-curve**: Using the limiting average to predict or bound the rank of one specific twist or a small finite sample of twists. -> The statement is an asymptotic average over all parameters; it gives no pointwise bound and no rate of convergence at finite height.
- **anti-discriminant-ordering**: Applying the result to families ordered by fundamental discriminant or restricted to one sign. -> The counting convention differs from the original formulation, so the result does not transfer automatically.
- **anti-thin-families-moments**: Extending the conclusions to thin polynomial subfamilies or to higher analytic-rank moments. -> The moment corollary concerns algebraic ranks only and carries no uniformity beyond fixed parameters.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are asymptotic limit statements and analytic estimates with implied constants, proved by contour shifts, Poisson summation and probabilistic comparison. No finite procedure with explicit inputs and outputs is stated, and the constants are not made explicit enough for a finite computation to decide any theorem, so no executable reference with an independent oracle exists.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves asymptotic limits and tail bounds with constants that depend on the curve and are not computed anywhere. There is no interface, routine or data flow to turn into a library. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The independent random character model is a proof device for residue averages. It is not a simulation of any physical or game system, and it comes with no sampling procedure. |
| tools | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The unconditional limits give an asymptotic reference value against which a tool that samples signed squarefree twists of a fixed curve could sanity-check its empirical mean rank. This is a benchmark use only, because the paper gives no algorithm. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The counting bound on large-rank twists is an existence-type estimate with unspecified constants. It states no procedure, so no pseudocode or complexity can be extracted from it. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite auxiliary family of curves is a proof-level reduction object. It does not define a stored structure, and the paper gives no operations or costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The uniformity in the derivative order is an analytic statement about parameters growing with the height. It has no relation to parallel or fixed-function computation. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Both signs of the twist parameter are averaged over the whole family. This gives no guarantee about the rank of any individual curve chosen for a protocol, and no hardness assumption is involved. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The probability model of independent character values is a device for comparing integer averages with expectations. It is not a learning setup, and no estimation from data occurs. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper states that it does not optimise its exponents. Nothing is posed as an objective with feasible solutions, so no optimisation contract exists. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | All parameters are fixed before the height tends to infinity. There is no uniformity for parameters varying with the height, and no notion of nodes, messages or fault tolerance. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The argument is a long informal analytic proof, and no machine-checked version is given. It also fixes a counting convention that differs from discriminant ordering, which blocks transfer to nearby settings. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **lim-asymptotic-only** (formal_scope): The mean theorem is a limit statement as the height tends to infinity over the whole family. It gives no pointwise value and no rate of convergence for any finite height. Blocked use: ['Predicting or bounding the rank of one specific twist from the limit', 'Treating a finite-height deviation of an empirical mean as a contradiction']
- **lim-counting-convention** (hypothesis): The counting parameter is the signed squarefree integer ordered by absolute value, not the quadratic-field discriminant. Blocked use: ['Claiming the mean for families ordered by fundamental discriminant', 'Restricting to one sign of the twist parameter and keeping the conclusion']
- **lim-density-input** (hypothesis): The deduction of the mean uses the companion density statement for ranks zero and one as its only imported input. The mean theorem is therefore not independent of that companion result. Blocked use: ['Citing the mean as self-contained without the companion density theorem']
- **lim-tail-constants** (nonconstructive): The tail bound has constants and a threshold that depend on the curve and are not made explicit. Blocked use: ['Using the bound as an explicit numerical estimate at a given R and Y', 'Extracting a runtime or sample-size guarantee']
- **lim-algebraic-only** (formal_scope): The moment corollary concerns algebraic ranks only, for fixed parameters, and says nothing about thin polynomial subfamilies. Blocked use: ['Inferring higher analytic-rank moments', 'Applying the moment limits to thin polynomial subfamilies', 'Letting the exponent or moment order vary with Y']
- **lim-derivative-range** (edge_case): The counting bound holds only for the derivative order between the base value plus one and a fixed power of the logarithm of the height, and only for sufficiently large dyadic heights. Blocked use: ['Applying the count to larger k', 'Applying the count at small heights']
- **lim-pointwise-rank** (approximation): The pointwise rank bound is of logarithmic size in the height with an implied constant depending on the curve, so it is crude for any single twist. Blocked use: ['Using it to certify a small rank for an individual twist']
- **lim-admissible-reduction** (hypothesis): The analytic estimates are proved for admissible parameters in a finite family and transferred to arbitrary parameters only for sums of nonnegative terms, using bounded multiplicity. Blocked use: ['Transferring signed or non-monotone quantities to arbitrary twists by the same reduction']
- **lim-formal-status** (unknown): No machine-checked formalisation is given; the proof is an informal analytic argument. Blocked use: ['Describing the result as formally verified']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every elliptic curve over ℚ, we prove that the average analytic rank of its quadratic twists tends to 1/2 when signed squarefree twist parameters are ordered by absolute value. This resolves Goldfeld's mean analytic-rank conjecture in this counting convention.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Goldfeld’s mean-rank conjecture predicts that the average central order of vanishing in a quadratic-twist family is $1/2$ (Goldfeld 1979, 113, Conjecture (B)) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Goldfeld1979](#ref-Goldfeld1979)].
